
---

## Process Architecture — How it Starts

```
python application/Launcher.py
         │
         ├─ _find_free_port(PORT, default=8001)       Backend port
         ├─ _find_free_port(CDSW_APP_PORT, default=8100)  UI port
         │
         ├─ subprocess.Popen(                         Sub-process 1
         │    [sys.executable, "-m", "uvicorn",
         │     "application.main:app",
         │     "--host", _BIND_HOST,                  ← 0.0.0.0 on Linux
         │     "--port", backend_port],               ← 127.0.0.1 on Windows
         │    creationflags=CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP   ← Windows only
         │  )
         │
         ├─ _wait_for_backend(url, timeout=60)        Polls GET /health
         │
         ├─ subprocess.Popen(                         Sub-process 2
         │    [sys.executable, "run_ui.py"],
         │    env={CB_BACKEND_URL, CDSW_APP_PORT}
         │  )
         │
         └─ Watchdog loop (time.sleep(1))
              poll() both processes every second
              Ctrl+C → CTRL_BREAK_EVENT (Win) / SIGTERM (Linux)
```

The backend (`application/main.py`) initialises its own LangGraph pipeline on startup via the
FastAPI `lifespan` handler:

```
FastAPI lifespan (startup)
    │
    ├─ get_settings()                   ← Reads .env + TOML defaults
    ├─ ModelLoaderService(settings)     ← Connects to DLH or ICA
    ├─ model_service.load()             ← Validates provider credentials
    ├─ build_nl2sql_graph(              ← Compiles the StateGraph once
    │    model_service, db_path)
    └─ ComplianceProcessor(compiled_graph)
```

---

## Six-Node LangGraph Pipeline

The compiled `StateGraph` is built once at startup and invoked per request.
All six nodes share a single `AgentState` TypedDict that LangGraph threads through.

```
[START]
   │
   ▼
┌─────────────────────────┐
│  Node 1: IntentChecker  │  Rule-based — NO LLM
│  intent_checker.py      │
└─────────────────────────┘
   │
   ├─ intent_ok=False ───────────────────────► clarify_node ──► [END]
   │                                           Returns clarification_needed
   │ intent_ok=True
   ▼
┌─────────────────────────┐
│  Node 2: SchemaChecker  │  Rule-based — NO LLM
│  schema_checker.py      │
└─────────────────────────┘
   │
   ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Node 3: SQLWriterAgent          ★ ONLY LLM CALL            │ ◄──────────┐
│  sql_writer_agent.py                                        │            │ retry
│  ContextBuilder builds full prompt from:                    │            │
│    · _SYSTEM_ROLE         (sql_writer.toml [system])        │            │
│    · _RULES               (sql_writer.toml [rules])         │            │
│    · table description    (databases.toml)                  │            │
│    · column definitions   (databases.toml)                  │            │
│    · schema_context       (SchemaChecker output)            │            │
│    · few-shot examples    (databases.toml [few_shots])      │            │
│    · retry feedback       (SQLReviewer / SQLValidator)      │            │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘            │
   │                                                                        │
   ▼                                                                        │
┌─────────────────────────┐                                                 │
│  Node 4: SQLReviewer    │  Rule-based — NO LLM                           │
│  sql_reviewer.py        │                                                 │
│  RetryHandler tracks    │                                                 │
│  retry_count            │                                                 │
└─────────────────────────┘                                                 │
   │                                                                        │
   ├─ error (max retries hit) ──────────────────────────────► executor ──► [END]
   │                                                                        │
   ├─ review_passed=False ──────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
   │  (increments retry_count, sets review_feedback)                        │
   │                                                                        │
   │ review_passed=True                                                     │
   ▼                                                                        │
┌─────────────────────────┐                                                 │
│  Node 4.5: SQLValidator │  sqlglot AST — NO LLM                          │
│  sql_validator.py       │                                                 │
└─────────────────────────┘                                                 │
   │                                                                        │
   ├─ sqlglot_valid=False ──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   │  (increments retry_count, sets review_feedback = sqlglot parse error)
   │
   │ sqlglot_valid=True (also normalises SQL to SQLite canonical form)
   ▼
┌─────────────────────────┐
│  Node 5: ExecutorAgent  │  Read-only SQLite — NO LLM
│  executor_agent.py      │
│  BEGIN DEFERRED;        │
│  fetchmany(max_rows);   │
│  ROLLBACK unconditional │
└─────────────────────────┘
   │
   ▼
[END]  →  result_rows, result_columns, result_summary, stages[]
```

---

### Node 1 — IntentChecker

**File:** [`genai/nl2sql_agent/intent_checker.py`](../genai/nl2sql_agent/intent_checker.py)
**LLM:** No — pure rule-based classification.

Four sequential checks, short-circuiting on first failure:

| # | Check | Trigger condition | Outcome |
|---|-------|-------------------|---------|
| 1 | Empty question | `question == ""` | `intent_ok=False`, clarification message |
| 2 | Disallowed action word | Token in `{DELETE, DROP, INSERT, UPDATE, TRUNCATE, ALTER, CREATE, …}` | `intent_ok=False`, blocked word named in message |
| 3 | Too short / meaningless | Fewer than 3 meaningful tokens (stop-words removed) AND no domain metric term | `intent_ok=False` |
| 4 | Vague / ambiguous | Pure vague phrase OR ambiguous pronoun with no domain term | `intent_ok=False` |
| — | All checks pass | — | `intent_ok=True`, `interpreted_question` with trailing `?` |

All user-facing messages come from `genai/prompts/intent_checker.toml`.
Domain vocabulary (`_METRIC_TERMS`) includes: `age, kyc, balance, maturity, product, branch,
circle, senior, account, rate, deposit, customer, flag, date, interest, tenor, count, sum…`

---

### Node 2 — SchemaChecker

**File:** [`genai/nl2sql_agent/schema_checker.py`](../genai/nl2sql_agent/schema_checker.py)
**LLM:** No — SQLite `PRAGMA` introspection + keyword scoring.

**Database selection algorithm:**

1. Glob all `**/*.db` files under `data/comp_db/`.
2. Score each file: +1 if the DB stem appears in the question, +2 per table name match, +1 per column name match.
3. Select the highest-scoring file (default: `COMP_REPORTS.db`).

**Schema context building** (injected into LLM prompt):

```
## Database: `COMP_REPORTS`

### Table: `COMP_TBL`

| # | Column | Type | PK | Not Null |
|---|--------|------|----|----------|
| 0 | BRCD   | TEXT |    |    ✓    |
…

**Sample rows from `COMP_TBL`:**
```
BRCD, CUST_NBR, …
1234, 9876543, …
```

**Column descriptions for `COMP_TBL` (from databases.toml):**
- `BRCD`: Branch code or branch number
…
```

Configuration (`sample_row_limit`, header templates) comes from `genai/prompts/schema_checker.toml`.
Business column descriptions come from `genai/config/databases.toml` via `registry.get_table_meta()`.

---

### Node 3 — SQLWriterAgent (only LLM call)

**File:** [`genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_writer_agent.py`](../genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_writer_agent.py)
**LLM:** **Yes** — the only node that calls `ModelLoaderService.generate()`.

**ContextBuilder** assembles the full prompt from:

| Source | Content |
|--------|---------|
| `sql_writer.toml [system.role]` | System role persona |
| `sql_writer.toml [rules.items]` | Ordered generation rules (SELECT-only, delimiter tags, column restrictions …) |
| `databases.toml [tables.COMP_TBL.description]` | Plain-English table description |
| `databases.toml [tables.COMP_TBL.columns]` | Per-column business definitions |
| `SchemaChecker output` | Live Markdown schema block (PRAGMA-sourced) |
| `databases.toml [tables.COMP_TBL.few_shots]` | 3+ worked examples |
| `review_feedback` (retry only) | Corrective feedback from Node 4 / 4.5 |

The LLM must wrap SQL in `<begin_sql_response>…<end_sql_response>` delimiter tags.
`ModelLoaderService.extract_sql()` strips these before passing on.

**SQL clean-up helpers** (post-extraction):
- `strip_comments()` — removes `--` inline and `/* */` block comments
- `normalise_whitespace()` — collapses horizontal whitespace runs
- `strip_trailing_semicolon()` — removes trailing `;`
- `optimise()` — chains all three

---

### Node 4 — SQLReviewer

**File:** [`genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_reviewer.py`](../genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_reviewer.py)
**LLM:** No — regex + live PRAGMA check.

**RetryHandler** enforces `max_retries=3` (configurable in `build_nl2sql_graph()`).
Once `retry_count >= max_retries`, sets `error` and routes directly to the Executor to surface the failure.

Four validation checks in order:

| # | Check | Failure action |
|---|-------|----------------|
| Guard | `retry_count >= max_retries` | `error` set, route to Executor (surface failure) |
| Guard | Empty SQL | `retry_count + 1`, route to Writer |
| 1 | Disallowed keywords (`INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `DROP`, `TRUNCATE`, `ALTER`, `CREATE`, `REPLACE`, `PRAGMA`, `ATTACH`, `DETACH`, `VACUUM`) | Feedback with offending keyword, route to Writer |
| 2 | Must start with `SELECT` | Feedback: "query must begin with SELECT" |
| 3 | Must contain `FROM` clause | Feedback: "missing FROM clause" |
| 4 | All referenced tables must exist in the live SQLite database | Lists unknown table names in feedback |

All feedback strings come from `genai/prompts/sql_review.toml`.

---

### Node 4.5 — SQLValidator

**File:** [`genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_validator.py`](../genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_validator.py)
**LLM:** No — `sqlglot` AST parser.

Three checks:

| # | Check | Failure action |
|---|-------|----------------|
| 1 | `sqlglot.parse()` must succeed (no `ParseError`) | `sqlglot_valid=False`, `retry_count+1`, route to Writer |
| 2 | Statement type must be `SELECT` (AST-level) | Same as above |
| 3 | Canonical SQLite dialect round-trip | On success, replaces `sql_query` with canonical form |

If `sqlglot` is not installed, this node passes automatically (allowing graceful degradation).
All errors set `review_feedback` so the Writer sees the reason on retry.

---

### Node 5 — ExecutorAgent

**File:** [`genai/nl2sql_agent/executor_agent.py`](../genai/nl2sql_agent/executor_agent.py)
**LLM:** No — read-only SQLite execution.

**Row-limit policy** (from `genai/config/query.toml`):

| Setting | Default | Effect |
|---------|---------|--------|
| `enforce_default_limit` | `true` | Auto-appends `LIMIT 10` if SQL has no `LIMIT` clause |
| `default_limit` | `10` | The injected row limit when above is true |
| `max_display_rows` | `500` | Hard cap via `fetchmany(500)` regardless of SQL LIMIT |

**Read-only guarantee:**

```python
conn = sqlite3.connect(str(db_path), check_same_thread=False)
conn.execute("BEGIN DEFERRED")           # deferred = read-only intent
cursor = conn.execute(effective_sql)
rows   = cursor.fetchmany(max_display_rows)
# ... always in finally:
conn.rollback()                          # unconditional rollback
conn.close()
```

**Result formatting helpers** (in the same file):
- `format_summary()` — one-sentence summary; detects aggregates (single cell = COUNT result)
- `to_markdown_table()` — GitHub-Flavoured Markdown table (max 20 display rows)
- `to_csv()` — CSV with header row
- `extract_scalar()` — pulls single value from aggregate result

---

## AgentState — Shared Pipeline State

`AgentState` is a `TypedDict(total=False)` in [`genai/nl2sql_agent/state.py`](../genai/nl2sql_agent/state.py).
LangGraph merges partial updates — each node writes only the keys it owns.
The `stages` list uses an `Annotated[List[str], operator.add]` reducer so LangGraph appends
entries from every node rather than overwriting.

| Field | Owner node | Type | Description |
|-------|-----------|------|-------------|
| `question` | Input | `str` | Raw user question |
| `question_id` | Input | `str \| None` | Caller-supplied idempotency key |
| `interpreted_question` | IntentChecker | `str` | Normalised question (trailing `?` ensured) |
| `intent_ok` | IntentChecker | `bool` | `True` → continue; `False` → clarify |
| `clarification_needed` | IntentChecker | `str \| None` | Message shown when intent is unclear |
| `db_path` | SchemaChecker | `str` | Absolute path to the selected `.db` file |
| `schema_context` | SchemaChecker | `str` | Markdown schema block injected into LLM prompt |
| `sql_query` | SQLWriterAgent / SQLValidator | `str` | Generated (then canonicalised) SQL |
| `sql_review_passed` | SQLReviewer | `bool` | `True` = safety checks passed |
| `review_feedback` | SQLReviewer / SQLValidator | `str \| None` | Corrective message for retry cycle |
| `retry_count` | SQLReviewer / SQLValidator | `int` | Number of writer → reviewer cycles so far |
| `sqlglot_valid` | SQLValidator | `bool` | AST parse result |
| `sqlglot_error` | SQLValidator | `str \| None` | Parse error detail |
| `result_rows` | ExecutorAgent | `list[dict]` | Query result rows |
| `result_columns` | ExecutorAgent | `list[str]` | Column names in result order |
| `result_summary` | ExecutorAgent | `str` | Human-readable result summary |
| `error` | Any node | `str \| None` | Pipeline-level error (surfaces to API) |
| `stages` | All (reducer appends) | `list[str]` | Ordered audit trail of each node's outcome |

---

## End-to-End Request Data Flow

```
Browser / curl
    │
    │  POST /api/v1/compliance/orchestrate
    │  {"question": "How many accounts have KYC flag N?", "question_id": "req-001"}
    ▼
application/main.py  (FastAPI endpoint compliance_orchestrate)
    │  checks _compliance_processor is not None (503 if startup failed)
    │
    ▼
genai/core/processor.py  (ComplianceProcessor.process_query_with_orchestration)
    │  question_id = request.question_id or uuid4()
    │  initial_state = AgentState(question=..., question_id=..., stages=[])
    │
    ▼
compiled LangGraph StateGraph  (genai/nl2sql_agent/graph.py)
    │
    ├── IntentChecker.run(state)
    │     → state: {interpreted_question, intent_ok, clarification_needed, stages+}
    │     if intent_ok=False → clarify_node → END
    │
    ├── SchemaChecker.run(state)
    │     scores all .db files, selects best
    │     PRAGMA introspects tables + columns + sample rows
    │     merges databases.toml column descriptions
    │     → state: {db_path, schema_context, stages+}
    │
    ├── SQLWriterAgent.run(state)
    │     ContextBuilder assembles full prompt (TOML + registry + schema_context)
    │     ModelLoaderService.generate(prompt)  ← HTTP call to DLH or ICA
    │     ModelLoaderService.extract_sql(raw_output)
    │     → state: {sql_query, retry_count, stages+}
    │
    ├── SQLReviewer.run(state)
    │     if retry_count >= max_retries → sets error → route to executor
    │     4 sequential checks (keywords, SELECT, FROM, table existence)
    │     if failed → retry_count+1, review_feedback → route to SQLWriterAgent
    │     → state: {sql_review_passed, review_feedback, retry_count, stages+}
    │
    ├── SQLValidator.run(state)
    │     sqlglot.parse(sql_query)
    │     if parse error / non-SELECT → retry_count+1 → route to SQLWriterAgent
    │     if passed → sql_query = canonical SQLite form
    │     → state: {sqlglot_valid, sqlglot_error, sql_query, stages+}
    │
    └── ExecutorAgent.run(state)
          applies LIMIT from query.toml if missing
          BEGIN DEFERRED; fetchmany(500); ROLLBACK
          format_summary(rows, columns)
          → state: {result_rows, result_columns, result_summary, error, stages+}
    │
    ▼
ComplianceProcessor._map_state_to_response(question_id, final_state)
    │
    ▼
ComplianceOrchestrationResponse {
    question_id, interpreted_question,
    needs_clarification, clarification_question,
    sql_query, df_output, result_columns,
    result_summary, stages[], error
}
    │
    ▼
Browser / curl  (JSON 200)
```

---

## Graph Routing Logic

Routing is controlled by three pure functions in `graph.py`:

```python
# After Node 1 (IntentChecker)
def _route_after_intent(state):
    return "schema_checker" if state["intent_ok"] else "clarify_node"

# After Node 4 (SQLReviewer)
def _route_after_reviewer(state):
    if state.get("error"):        return "executor_agent"   # max retries → surface error
    if state["sql_review_passed"]: return "sql_validator"   # passed → validate
    return "sql_writer_agent"                               # failed → retry writer

# After Node 4.5 (SQLValidator)
def _route_after_validator(state):
    return "executor_agent" if state["sqlglot_valid"] else "sql_writer_agent"
```

Fixed edges: `START→intent`, `schema→writer`, `writer→reviewer`, `executor→END`, `clarify→END`.

---

## Three-Layer Read-Only Enforcement

```
Layer 0 — IntentChecker  (Node 1)
    Scans the raw USER QUESTION for DELETE/DROP/INSERT/UPDATE/TRUNCATE/ALTER …
    → halts immediately — no LLM call, no SQL generated, no database touched.
    Triggered by: _DISALLOWED_ACTION_WORDS in intent_checker.py

Layer 1 — SQLReviewer  (Node 4)
    Scans the LLM-GENERATED SQL with \bKEYWORD\b regex for the same words
    plus CREATE/REPLACE/PRAGMA/ATTACH/DETACH/VACUUM.
    → sends corrective feedback to Writer, retries up to max_retries times.
    → after max_retries, routes to Executor which surfaces the error.

Layer 2 — ExecutorAgent  (Node 5)
    Opens SQLite with BEGIN DEFERRED transaction.
    Calls conn.rollback() unconditionally in the finally block.
    → even if a mutating statement somehow bypassed layers 0 and 1,
      the transaction is always rolled back — the database is physically safe.
```

---

## LLM Provider Architecture

```
ModelLoaderService  (genai/core/model.py)
    │
    ├── provider = "dlh"
    │       OpenAI-compatible Python client (openai package)
    │       base_url  = settings.dlh_base_url   (env: MODEL__DLH_BASE_URL)
    │       api_key   = settings.dlh_api_key    (env: MODEL__DLH_API_KEY)
    │       verify_ssl = settings.dlh_verify_ssl (default: False)
    │       Sends:  temperature, max_tokens always
    │
    └── provider = "ica"
            IBM Consulting Advantage (ICA)
            api_key   = settings.ica_api_key    (env: MODEL__ICA_API_KEY)
            namespace = settings.ica_namespace  (default: "chat-models")
            base_url  built as:
              host + _ICA_NAMESPACE_PATHS[namespace]
              paths: /ica/v1/chat-models/  (default)
                     /ica/v1/agents/
                     /ica/v1/assistants/
                     /ica/v1/digital-workforce/
            Sends:  temperature/max_tokens only if ica_send_temperature=True
```

**Hot-swap** via `POST /api/v1/config/llm`:
- Builds a new `ModelLoaderService` with the submitted credentials.
- Calls `model_service.test_connection()` (sends `"Reply with OK."` probe).
- Only on success, atomically replaces the active `_model_service` and `_compliance_processor`.

**Inference helpers** (also in `model.py`):
- `generate_with_retry(model_service, prompt, retries=2)` — retries with `time.sleep` between attempts
- `extract_text_between(text, start, end)` — generic delimiter extraction
- `truncate_prompt(prompt, max_chars=8000)` — cuts from the middle, preserving head + tail context

---

## Configuration System

Settings are resolved in this priority order (highest wins):

```
1. Environment variables (e.g. MODEL__DLH_API_KEY=...)
2. .env file (project root — never committed)
3. TOML config files in genai/config/  (committed defaults)
4. Pydantic Field(default=...) values  (hard-coded fallback)
```

`AppSettings` (Pydantic `BaseSettings`) is instantiated once as a module-level singleton
in `genai/utils/config.py` and retrieved everywhere via `get_settings()`.

**Nested settings separator:** `__` (double underscore)
Example: `MODEL__DLH_API_KEY` sets `settings.model.dlh_api_key`.

### TOML config file map

| File | Controls |
|------|---------|
| `application.toml` | `application_name`, `host`, `port`, `debug`, `log_level`, `log_dir` |
| `database.toml` | `db_type`, `db_folder`, `db_name` |
| `databases.toml` | Table registry: descriptions, columns, few-shots per table per DB |
| `llm.toml` | Default `provider`, `model_name`, `max_new_tokens`, `temperature` |
| `logging.toml` | `dictConfig`-compatible handler/formatter/level config + rotation |
| `query.toml` | `enforce_default_limit`, `default_limit`, `max_display_rows` |
| `ui.toml` | UI proxy `backend_url` override |

---

## Prompt System (TOML-driven)

Zero prompt strings are hardcoded in Python. All user-facing messages and LLM prompt templates
live in `genai/prompts/*.toml` and are accessed via `registry.get_prompt(filename, *keys)`.

**Access pattern:**

```python
from genai.utils.registry import get_prompt, load_prompt

# Single value with fallback
msg = get_prompt("intent_checker.toml", "messages", "empty_question",
                 default="No question was provided.")

# Full file dict (e.g. for template strings or rule lists)
_WRITER = load_prompt("sql_writer.toml")
_RULES  = _WRITER.get("rules", {}).get("items", [])
```

Files are parsed once at import time and cached in `_prompt_cache` (module-level dict).

### Prompt file responsibilities

| File | Used by | Contains |
|------|---------|---------|
| `intent_checker.toml` | `IntentChecker` | User-facing messages: empty, disallowed, too_short, clarification |
| `schema_checker.toml` | `SchemaChecker` | `sample_row_limit`, section header templates, error messages |
| `sql_writer.toml` | `SQLWriterAgent` / `ContextBuilder` | `[system]` role, `[rules]` list, `[prompt]` template, `[retry]` feedback header |
| `sql_review.toml` | `SQLReviewer` | `[feedback]` corrective hints → writer; `[user_error]` messages → API |
| `compliance.toml` | Legacy | Compliance domain messages |
| `report_generation.toml` | Report pipeline | Report output templates |
| `system.toml` | Shared | Global system-level messages |
| `common.toml` | Shared | SQL delimiter tags reference |

---

## Database Registry (databases.toml)

`genai/config/databases.toml` is the **single file to edit** when adding a new database or table.
It is parsed once at import time by `registry._build_db_registry()` into a flat
`{table_name: metadata}` dict.

**Structure:**

```toml
[databases.comp_reports]
db_file = "data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db"

[databases.comp_reports.tables.COMP_TBL]
description   = "Compliance table for term-deposit products …"
filter_query  = "SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE ACCT_BRNCH IN ({branch_code})"

[databases.comp_reports.tables.COMP_TBL.columns]
BRCD     = "Branch code or branch number"
CUST_NBR = "Customer number — unique identifier"
…

[databases.comp_reports.tables.COMP_TBL.few_shots]
examples = """
Question: Show top 10 balances.
Response:
<begin_sql_response>
SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL ORDER BY END_OF_DAY_BAL DESC LIMIT 10;
<end_sql_response>
"""
```

**Registry access API:**

```python
from genai.utils.registry import (
    get_table_meta,    # full metadata dict for a table
    get_few_shots,     # few-shot string for a table
    get_all_tables,    # sorted list of all registered tables
    list_databases,    # sorted list of database stems
)
```

---

## Exception Hierarchy

```
ComplianceBotError (base — genai/core/exceptions.py)
    │
    ├── ModelLoadError          — LLM / tokeniser failed to load
    ├── ModelInferenceError     — unexpected output during inference
    ├── LLMProviderError        — provider HTTP / auth error
    ├── LLMTimeoutError         — provider call exceeded timeout
    ├── UnknownProviderError    — unrecognised provider name
    │
    ├── SqlGenerationError      — pipeline cannot produce valid SQL
    ├── SqlExecutionError       — generated SQL fails to execute
    │
    ├── DatabaseConnectionError — cannot connect to database
    ├── RecordNotFoundError     — expected record absent
    │
    ├── DataIngestionError      — raw data cannot be ingested
    └── SchemaValidationError   — payload fails schema validation
```

All domain exceptions are caught in `main.py`'s `compliance_bot_exception_handler` and mapped to
a `500 Internal Server Error` JSON response. Stack traces are never leaked to API clients.

---

## Key Design Decisions

| Decision | Rationale |
|----------|-----------|
| **No hardcoded prompts in Python** | All prompts in `genai/prompts/*.toml` — edit wording without touching code or redeploying |
| **No hardcoded table metadata in Python** | All in `genai/config/databases.toml` — add a new database by editing one file |
| **No hardcoded row limits in Python** | All in `genai/config/query.toml` — change limits without redeployment |
| **LangGraph `StateGraph`** | Deterministic node routing, built-in retry edges, observable `stages[]` audit trail |
| **`sqlglot` validation (Node 4.5)** | AST-level check catches malformed SQL that regex-based Node 4 misses |
| **`BEGIN DEFERRED` + unconditional rollback** | Database-level read-only guarantee independent of all application code |
| **PRAGMA live introspection (Node 2)** | Schema context is always accurate — no manual schema files to keep in sync |
| **Single LLM call per request** | Only Node 3 calls the LLM; all other nodes are deterministic, fast, and testable without a model |
| **Flat `AgentState` TypedDict** | Zero coupling between nodes — each reads what it needs, writes what it owns |
| **TOML prompt cache** | Prompt files are parsed once at import time; no disk I/O per request |
| **OS-aware Launcher** | `_BIND_HOST`, `CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP`, `CTRL_BREAK_EVENT` — works correctly on both Linux/CDSW and Windows |

FILE: docs\configuration.md
####################################################################################################

# Configuration Reference

All configuration is split across TOML files in `genai/config/` and environment variables / `.env`. Environment variables always take precedence over TOML defaults.

---

## Environment Variables (`.env`)

Copy `.env.example` to `.env` before first run:

```bash
cp .env.example .env
```

### Application settings

| Variable | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `HOST` | `0.0.0.0` | FastAPI bind host |
| `PORT` | `8000` | FastAPI bind port |
| `DEBUG` | `false` | Enable uvicorn hot-reload (dev only) |
| `LOG_LEVEL` | `INFO` | Root log level: `DEBUG` / `INFO` / `WARNING` / `ERROR` |
| `LOG_DIR` | `data/logs` | Directory for rotating log files |

### Database settings

| Variable | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `DATABASE__DB_FOLDER` | `data/comp_db` | Folder containing `.db` files |
| `DATABASE__DB_NAME` | `COMP_REPORTS.db` | Default database filename |

### LLM settings

| Variable | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `MODEL__PROVIDER` | `dlh` | Active provider: `dlh` |
| `MODEL__MODEL_NAME` | `google/gemma-4-31b-it` | Model name on the DLH inference server |
| `MODEL__MODEL_MAX_NEW_TOKENS` | `512` | Max tokens to generate per call |
| `MODEL__MODEL_TEMPERATURE` | `0.1` | Sampling temperature |
| `MODEL__DLH_BASE_URL` | `http://10.190.236.15:9000/api/v1` | DLH inference server base URL |
| `MODEL__DLH_API_KEY` | `EMPTY` | DLH bearer token |
| `MODEL__DLH_VERIFY_SSL` | `false` | Verify DLH TLS certificates |

---

## `genai/config/query.toml` — Row-limit policy

Controls how many rows the executor returns. **Edit this file to change limit behaviour without redeployment.**

```toml
[query]
enforce_default_limit = true   # true | false
default_limit         = 10     # integer, rows injected when SQL has no LIMIT
max_display_rows      = 500    # integer, hard fetchmany() ceiling
```

| Key | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `enforce_default_limit` | bool | `true` | When `true`: if the LLM SQL has no `LIMIT`, append `LIMIT <default_limit>` automatically |
| `default_limit` | int | `10` | Number of rows shown when no LIMIT is present and enforcement is on |
| `max_display_rows` | int | `500` | Absolute maximum rows returned regardless of any LIMIT clause |

**Behaviour matrix:**

| `enforce_default_limit` | SQL has LIMIT? | Rows returned |
|---|---|---|
| `true` | No | `default_limit` (e.g. 10) |
| `true` | Yes (`LIMIT 50`) | Up to 50 (capped at `max_display_rows`) |
| `false` | No | All rows (capped at `max_display_rows`) |
| `false` | Yes | Up to the specified LIMIT |

---

## `genai/config/databases.toml` — Database and table registry

**The only file to edit when adding a new database or table.**

See [adding-new-database.md](adding-new-database.md) for the full step-by-step guide.

Structure:

```toml
[databases.<DB_STEM>]
db_file     = "relative/path/to/file.db"
description = "Human-readable description (internal only)"

[databases.<DB_STEM>.tables.<TABLE_NAME>]
description  = "Used in LLM prompt as table summary"
filter_query = "Optional parameterised filter template"

[databases.<DB_STEM>.tables.<TABLE_NAME>.columns]
COL_NAME = "Business-friendly description (appears in LLM prompt)"

[databases.<DB_STEM>.tables.<TABLE_NAME>.few_shots]
examples = """
Question: ...
Response:
<begin_sql_response>
SELECT ...
<end_sql_response>
"""
```

---

## `genai/config/llm.toml` — LLM provider defaults

Provides default values. Overridden by `.env` / environment variables.

```toml
[llm]
provider              = "dlh"
model_name            = "google/gemma-4-31b-it"
model_max_new_tokens  = 512
model_temperature     = 0.1

[llm.dlh]
base_url   = "http://10.190.236.15:9000/api/v1"
api_key    = "EMPTY"
verify_ssl = false
```

---

## `genai/config/application.toml` — Application settings

```toml
[application]
name        = "COMPLIANCE-BOT (UI + LLM Hybrid)"
page_config = "SBI COMPLIANCE-BOT"
version     = "2.0.0"

[server]
host      = "0.0.0.0"
port      = 8000
debug     = false
log_level = "INFO"
log_dir   = "data/logs"
```

---

## `genai/config/logging.toml` — Logging configuration

Controls the rotating file handler and console handler.

```toml
[logging.handlers.rotating_file]
filename    = "data/logs/compliance_bot.log"
maxBytes    = 10485760    # 10 MB per file
backupCount = 10          # keep 10 backups

[logging.loggers.compliance_bot]
level = "DEBUG"
```

Log files are written to `data/logs/compliance_bot.log` and rotate automatically.

---

## `genai/config/database.toml` — Database path defaults

```toml
[database]
db_type   = "SQLite"
db_folder = "data/comp_db"
db_name   = "COMP_REPORTS.db"
```

These are the fallback values. Override with `DATABASE__DB_FOLDER` and `DATABASE__DB_NAME` in `.env`.

---

## `genai/config/ui.toml` — UI proxy settings

```toml
[ui]
backend_url = "http://0.0.0.0:8066"
```

Override with `CB_BACKEND_URL` environment variable.

---

## `genai/prompts/` — Prompt TOML files

All prompt templates are stored here. Edit these files to change what the LLM sees **without touching Python code**.

| File | Used by | Content |
|---|---|---|
| `sql_writer.toml` | `SQLWriterAgent` | Prompt structure, system role, rules |
| `sql_review.toml` | `SQLReviewAgent` | Feedback strings, user-facing error messages |
| `intent_checker.toml` | `IntentAgent` | Clarification messages, blocked-word message template |
| `schema_checker.toml` | `SchemaAgent` | Sample row limit |
| `compliance.toml` | Legacy | Old compliance schema (superseded by `databases.toml`) |
| `common.toml` | Shared | SQL delimiter tags (`<begin_sql_response>` / `<end_sql_response>`) |
| `system.toml` | Application | System-level descriptions |
| `report_generation.toml` | Future | Report template (not yet active) |

---

## Launcher environment variables

| Variable | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `PORT` | `8001` | FastAPI backend port (auto-increments if busy) |
| `CDSW_APP_PORT` | `8100` | Standalone UI proxy port (auto-increments if busy) |
| `CB_BACKEND_URL` | set automatically | URL the UI proxy uses to reach the backend |

FILE: docs\database-schema.md
####################################################################################################

# Database Schema Reference

## COMP_REPORTS.db — `COMP_TBL`

The default compliance database (`data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db`) contains one table: `COMP_TBL`.

It stores banking term-deposit compliance records for senior-citizen customer segmentation and KYC tracking.

**Seeder:** `python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset --count 2000`

---

## Column Reference (22 columns)

| Column | SQLite Type | Description | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BRCD` | TEXT | Branch code or branch number | 4-digit alphanumeric |
| `CUST_NBR` | TEXT | Customer number — unique identifier assigned in the banking system | |
| `CUST_NBR_CHKDGT` | TEXT | Customer number check digit | Validation digit |
| `ACCT_NBR` | TEXT | Unique identifier for a specific bank account | |
| `ACCT_NBR_CHKDGT` | TEXT | Account number check digit | Validation digit |
| `ACCT_BRNCH` | TEXT | Account branch — branch where the account is maintained | May differ from `BRCD` |
| `BRTH_DT` | TEXT | Birth date — customer date of birth | Format: `YYYY-MM-DD` |
| `AGE_YRS` | INTEGER | Age in years — calculated from birth date | Used for senior-citizen segmentation |
| `SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG` | INTEGER | Senior citizen flag | `1` = senior citizen (≥60), `0` = not |
| `KYC_FLG` | TEXT | KYC compliance flag | `Y` = compliant, `N` = non-compliant |
| `END_OF_DAY_BAL` | REAL | End-of-day balance — closing balance at end of business day | INR |
| `ACCT_OPN_DT` | TEXT | Account open date — when the account was first opened | Format: `YYYY-MM-DD` |
| `MTRTY_DT` | TEXT | Maturity date — when the term deposit reaches maturity | Format: `YYYY-MM-DD` |
| `NEW_EFFCTV_RT` | REAL | New effective rate — updated interest rate applied to the account | Percentage |
| `PRDCT_ID` | TEXT | Product ID — identifier for the financial product | |
| `PF_FAC_REPT` | TEXT | Pension fund facility reporting indicator | |
| `PRODUCT_NAME` | TEXT | Human-readable name of the financial product | e.g. `SR CT DEPOSIT` |
| `BRANCHNAME` | TEXT | Full name of the branch | |
| `CIRCLENAME` | TEXT | Circle name — administrative region the branch belongs to | e.g. `MUMBAI`, `THANE` |
| `CIRCLECD` | TEXT | Circle code — code for the administrative region | |
| `EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT` | REAL | Expected effective interest rate for senior citizens on current product | Percentage |
| `ISMOD` | TEXT | MOD account flag | `YES` = Multi-Option Deposit, `NO` = standard |

---

## Sample Queries

```sql
-- Count all records
SELECT COUNT(*) AS total FROM COMP_TBL;

-- Senior citizen accounts
SELECT COUNT(*) AS senior_count FROM COMP_TBL WHERE SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG = 1;

-- KYC non-compliant accounts
SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE KYC_FLG = 'N' LIMIT 10;

-- Accounts in Mumbai circle
SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE UPPER(CIRCLENAME) LIKE '%MUMBAI%' LIMIT 10;

-- Top 10 by end-of-day balance
SELECT BRANCHNAME, CUST_NBR, END_OF_DAY_BAL
FROM COMP_TBL
ORDER BY END_OF_DAY_BAL DESC
LIMIT 10;

-- Accounts maturing in December 2025
SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL
WHERE MTRTY_DT BETWEEN '2025-12-01' AND '2025-12-31'
LIMIT 10;

-- Rate discrepancy (new rate lower than expected)
SELECT CUST_NBR, ACCT_NBR, NEW_EFFCTV_RT, EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT
FROM COMP_TBL
WHERE NEW_EFFCTV_RT < EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT
LIMIT 10;

-- Accounts by circle (aggregate)
SELECT CIRCLENAME, COUNT(*) AS account_count, AVG(END_OF_DAY_BAL) AS avg_balance
FROM COMP_TBL
GROUP BY CIRCLENAME
ORDER BY account_count DESC;
```

---

## Seeder statistics (2,000 rows)

| Metric | Approximate value |
|---|---|
| Total rows | 2,000 |
| Senior citizens (`SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG = 1`) | ~759 (38%) |
| KYC non-compliant (`KYC_FLG = 'N'`) | ~267 (13%) |
| MOD accounts (`ISMOD = 'YES'`) | ~40% |
| Circles covered | 13 cities, 20 circle codes |
| Products | 11 term-deposit products |
| Date range (maturity) | 2024–2028 |

---

## Filter query template

The `filter_query` in `databases.toml` provides a parameterised template for branch-filtered queries:

```sql
SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE ACCT_BRNCH IN ({branch_code})
```

Replace `{branch_code}` with a comma-separated list of branch codes (e.g., `'1001', '1002'`).

FILE: docs\folder-connections.md
####################################################################################################

# Folder Connections — How Every Folder Talks to Every Other Folder

> This document explains **what each folder does**, **how each folder connects to the others**,
> and **what is physically happening** at every connection point.
> Read it top-to-bottom for a complete picture, or jump to any folder heading.

---

## Quick Overview Diagram

```
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Browser / curl / CDSW                                               │
  └────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                           │ HTTP
  ┌────────────────────────▼─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  run_ui.py  /  application/run_ui.py                                 │
  │  Standalone HTTP proxy — serves HTML chat page                       │
  │  Proxies /api/* requests → FastAPI backend                           │
  └────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                           │ HTTP (proxied)
  ┌────────────────────────▼─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  application/main.py  (FastAPI / uvicorn)                            │
  │  All API endpoints live here                                         │
  │  Imports from genai/core/ and genai/nl2sql_agent/                    │
  └──────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────┬────────────┘
         │ imports                                          │ imports
  ┌──────▼──────────────┐                       ┌──────────▼────────────┐
  │  genai/core/        │                       │  genai/nl2sql_agent/  │
  │  processor.py       │◄──────────────────────│  graph.py + nodes     │
  │  model.py           │  builds + invokes      │                       │
  │  schemas.py         │  StateGraph            │                       │
  │  exceptions.py      │                       │                       │
  └──────┬──────────────┘                       └──────────┬────────────┘
         │ imports                                          │ imports
  ┌──────▼──────────────────────────────────────────────────▼────────────┐
  │  genai/utils/                                                         │
  │  config.py    ← settings singleton, constants                        │
  │  logger.py    ← logging factory (used by every module)               │
  │  registry.py  ← TOML prompt cache + DB metadata + SQLite helpers     │
  │  helpers.py   ← pure utility functions (no imports from genai/)      │
  └──────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         │ reads (file I/O, once at startup)
  ┌──────▼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  genai/config/  +  genai/prompts/                                     │
  │  TOML configuration and prompt files                                  │
  └──────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         │ SQL queries (read-only)
  ┌──────▼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  data/                                                                │
  │  comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db   ← SQLite database                         │
  │  logs/compliance_bot.log   ← rotating log file                       │
  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

## Folder: `application/`

**What it is:** The deployable server layer. Contains the FastAPI app, the process
manager, and the browser-facing UI proxy.

### `application/main.py` — FastAPI Backend

**Connects to:**

| Target | How | What happens |
|--------|-----|-------------|
| `genai/core/model.py` | Python import at startup | `ModelLoaderService` is constructed and `load()` is called to establish the DLH or ICA connection |
| `genai/nl2sql_agent/graph.py` | Python import at startup | `build_nl2sql_graph()` is called once; the compiled `StateGraph` object is held in memory for the lifetime of the process |
| `genai/core/processor.py` | Python import at startup | `ComplianceProcessor` wraps the compiled graph; all `/api/v1/compliance/*` requests go through it |
| `genai/core/schemas.py` | Python import at startup | Pydantic models validate every incoming request and outgoing response |
| `genai/core/exceptions.py` | Python import at startup | `ComplianceBotError` is registered as a FastAPI exception handler; domain errors return structured `500` JSON |
| `genai/utils/config.py` | Python import at startup | `get_settings()` provides host, port, application name, and LLM defaults |
| `genai/utils/logger.py` | Python import at startup | `get_logger("compliance_bot.application")` configures the application-level logger |

**Lifecycle:**

```
uvicorn starts → lifespan() runs →
    get_settings()
    ModelLoaderService.load()        ← connects to LLM provider
    build_nl2sql_graph()             ← compiles StateGraph (one-time)
    ComplianceProcessor created      ← holds the compiled graph
    [server accepts requests]
    [Ctrl+C / SIGTERM]
    model_service.unload()           ← releases provider client
```

---

### `application/Launcher.py` — Process Manager

**Connects to:**

| Target | How | What happens |
|--------|-----|-------------|
| `run_ui.py` (project root) | `subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "run_ui.py"])` | Spawns the UI proxy as a child process with `CB_BACKEND_URL` and `CDSW_APP_PORT` injected as environment variables |
| `application/main.py` | `subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-m", "uvicorn", "application.main:app", ...])` | Spawns the FastAPI backend as a child process |
| Backend health endpoint | `urllib.request.urlopen(f"{backend_url}/health")` | Polls `GET /health` every 0.5 s for up to 60 s to confirm the backend is ready before starting the UI |

**OS-specific behaviour:**

| Platform | Bind host | Ctrl+C | Shutdown signal |
|----------|-----------|--------|-----------------|
| Windows | `127.0.0.1` | `CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP` prevents propagation | `CTRL_BREAK_EVENT` |
| Linux / CDSW | `0.0.0.0` | Default | `SIGTERM` via `proc.terminate()` |

---

### `application/run_ui.py` and `run_ui.py` — UI Proxy

**What it does:** Serves the chat HTML page (defined inline in the same file as an
HTML string) and proxies every `/api/*` request to the FastAPI backend.

**Connects to:**

| Target | How | What happens |
|--------|-----|-------------|
| `application/fonts/` | `src: url("application/fonts/Urbanist-*.ttf")` in inline `<style>` | Browser fetches font files from the application fonts directory |
| FastAPI backend (`CB_BACKEND_URL`) | `urllib.request.urlopen()` for each proxied request | All `/api/*` HTTP calls from the browser are forwarded to the backend and the response is relayed back |

---

## Folder: `genai/`

The entire intelligence layer. Split into four sub-folders with clear responsibilities.

---

## Folder: `genai/core/`

**What it is:** The bridge between the FastAPI API layer and the LangGraph pipeline.
Also holds LLM lifecycle, Pydantic models, and the exception hierarchy.

### `genai/core/processor.py` — ComplianceProcessor

**Connects to:**

| Target | How | What happens |
|--------|-----|-------------|
| `genai/nl2sql_agent/graph.py` | Holds a reference to `compiled_graph` (passed into constructor) | `compiled_graph.invoke(initial_state)` is called for every request; LangGraph runs all six nodes |
| `genai/nl2sql_agent/state.py` | Python import | `AgentState` TypedDict is instantiated with the incoming question and handed to the graph |
| `genai/core/schemas.py` | Python import | Reads `ComplianceChatRequest`; builds `ComplianceOrchestrationResponse` from the final `AgentState` |
| `genai/utils/logger.py` | Python import | Request-level logging with question_id |

**What happens per request:**

```
request.question → AgentState{question, question_id, stages=[]}
                 → compiled_graph.invoke(state)
                 → final AgentState
                 → _map_state_to_response(question_id, final_state)
                 → ComplianceOrchestrationResponse
```

---

### `genai/core/model.py` — ModelLoaderService

**Connects to:**

| Target | How | What happens |
|--------|-----|-------------|
| DLH inference server | `openai.OpenAI(base_url=dlh_base_url, api_key=dlh_api_key)` | HTTP POST to the OpenAI-compatible `/chat/completions` endpoint |
| ICA (IBM Consulting Advantage) | `openai.OpenAI(base_url=ica_base_url + namespace_path, ...)` | HTTP POST to the ICA chat completions endpoint |
| `genai/utils/config.py` | Python import | Reads `ModelSettings` for provider, URL, key, model name, token limits |
| `genai/utils/logger.py` | Python import | Logs load, generate, extract, and unload events |

**Key methods used by the pipeline:**

```python
model_service.generate(prompt)      → raw LLM string response
model_service.extract_sql(response) → SQL extracted from delimiter tags
model_service.test_connection()     → sends "Reply with OK." probe
model_service.unload()              → releases OpenAI client object
```

---

### `genai/core/schemas.py` — Pydantic Models

**Connects to:** Used by both `application/main.py` (FastAPI request/response validation)
and `genai/core/processor.py` (result construction).

No I/O — pure Python data classes.

---

### `genai/core/exceptions.py` — Exception Hierarchy

**Connects to:** Imported by `application/main.py` (registered as exception handler),
`genai/core/model.py` (raises `ModelLoadError`, `ModelInferenceError`),
`genai/core/processor.py` (catches `ComplianceBotError`).

No I/O — pure Python class hierarchy.

---

## Folder: `genai/nl2sql_agent/`

**What it is:** The six-node LangGraph pipeline. Each `.py` file is one agent node,
except `graph.py` (wiring), `state.py` (shared state), and `main.py` (CLI debug).

### `genai/nl2sql_agent/graph.py` — StateGraph Factory

**Connects to:**

| Target | How | What happens |
|--------|-----|-------------|
| All six node files | Python imports | Instantiates `IntentChecker`, `SchemaChecker`, `SQLWriterAgent`, `SQLReviewer`, `SQLValidator`, `ExecutorAgent` |
| `genai/nl2sql_agent/state.py` | Python import | `StateGraph(AgentState)` — LangGraph uses the TypedDict as its state schema |
| `genai/utils/config.py` | Python import | `get_settings().database.db_path` supplies the default database path |
| `langgraph` package | Python import | `StateGraph`, `START`, `END` from LangGraph; `graph.compile()` returns the runnable graph |

---

### Node 1: `intent_checker.py` — IntentChecker

**Connects to:**

| Target | How | What happens |
|--------|-----|-------------|
| `genai/utils/registry.py` | `get_prompt("intent_checker.toml", ...)` | Retrieves user-facing clarification messages from the TOML prompt file |
| `genai/nl2sql_agent/state.py` | Reads + writes `AgentState` | Reads `question`, writes `interpreted_question`, `intent_ok`, `clarification_needed`, `stages` |
| `genai/utils/logger.py` | Python import | Logs each check outcome |

**No external I/O** — pure in-memory rule evaluation.

---

### Node 2: `schema_checker.py` — SchemaChecker

**Connects to:**

| Target | How | What happens |
|--------|-----|-------------|
| `data/comp_db/*.db` | `sqlite3.connect()` + `PRAGMA table_info()` + `SELECT * LIMIT N` | Opens every `.db` file found under `data_root`, reads table names, column definitions, and sample rows |
| `genai/utils/registry.py` | `get_prompt(...)` + `get_table_meta(...)` | Loads header/error message templates from `schema_checker.toml`; merges business column descriptions from `databases.toml` |
| `genai/nl2sql_agent/state.py` | Reads + writes `AgentState` | Reads `interpreted_question`, writes `db_path`, `schema_context`, `stages` |
| `genai/utils/logger.py` | Python import | Logs selected database name |

**What is built:** A multi-section Markdown string (schema context) containing:
- Table name header
- Formatted `PRAGMA table_info` column table
- Sample data rows
- Business column descriptions from `databases.toml`

This string is injected verbatim into the LLM prompt in Node 3.

---

### Node 3: `sql_writer_agent.py` — SQLWriterAgent + ContextBuilder

**Connects to:**

| Target | How | What happens |
|--------|-----|-------------|
| `genai/core/model.py` | `model_service.generate(prompt)` | HTTP call to DLH or ICA — the **only** external network call in the entire pipeline |
| `genai/core/model.py` | `model_service.extract_sql(raw_output)` | Extracts SQL from `<begin_sql_response>…<end_sql_response>` tags |
| `genai/utils/registry.py` | `load_prompt("sql_writer.toml")`, `get_table_meta()`, `get_few_shots()` | Loads system role, rules list, prompt template, column descriptions, and few-shot examples — all from TOML |
| `genai/utils/config.py` | `SQL_BEGIN_TAG`, `SQL_END_TAG`, `COMPLIANCE_TABLE_NAME` | String constants used in prompt and SQL extraction |
| `genai/nl2sql_agent/state.py` | Reads + writes `AgentState` | Reads `interpreted_question`, `schema_context`, `review_feedback`, `retry_count`; writes `sql_query`, `retry_count`, `stages`, `error` |

**What the `ContextBuilder` assembles:**

```
[INST] <<SYS>>
{system_role from sql_writer.toml}

Rules:
{numbered rules from sql_writer.toml}
<</SYS>>

=== Table Metadata: COMP_TBL ===
{description from databases.toml}

Columns:
{column definitions from databases.toml}

=== Live Database Schema ===
{schema_context from SchemaChecker — PRAGMA-sourced}

=== Few-shot Examples ===
{few-shot pairs from databases.toml}

[REVIEWER FEEDBACK — fix these issues]   ← only on retry
{review_feedback from SQLReviewer / SQLValidator}

Question: {interpreted_question}
Response:
[/INST]
```

---

### Node 4: `sql_reviewer.py` — SQLReviewer + RetryHandler

**Connects to:**

| Target | How | What happens |
|--------|-----|-------------|
| `data/comp_db/*.db` | `sqlite3.connect()` + `sqlite_master` query | Checks that every table referenced in the generated SQL actually exists in the database |
| `genai/utils/registry.py` | `get_prompt("sql_review.toml", ...)` | Loads all feedback message templates |
| `genai/nl2sql_agent/state.py` | Reads + writes `AgentState` | Reads `sql_query`, `db_path`, `retry_count`; writes `sql_review_passed`, `review_feedback`, `retry_count`, `stages`, `error` |
| `genai/utils/logger.py` | Python import | Logs each check result |

**RetryHandler** reads `retry_count` from state and increments it on failures.
When `retry_count >= max_retries`, sets `error` which causes the graph router to
send execution directly to Node 5 (Executor) to surface the failure.

---

### Node 4.5: `sql_validator.py` — SQLValidator

**Connects to:**

| Target | How | What happens |
|--------|-----|-------------|
| `sqlglot` package | `sqlglot.parse(sql, dialect="sqlite")` | Builds an AST for the SQL; raises `ParseError` on syntax problems |
| `genai/nl2sql_agent/state.py` | Reads + writes `AgentState` | Reads `sql_query`, `retry_count`; writes `sqlglot_valid`, `sqlglot_error`, `sql_query` (canonical), `retry_count`, `review_feedback`, `stages` |
| `genai/utils/logger.py` | Python import | Logs parse result |

**No file I/O, no network calls.** If `sqlglot` is not installed, the node returns
`sqlglot_valid=True` immediately (graceful degradation).

---

### Node 5: `executor_agent.py` — ExecutorAgent

**Connects to:**

| Target | How | What happens |
|--------|-----|-------------|
| `data/comp_db/*.db` (the specific file stored in `state["db_path"]`) | `sqlite3.connect(db_path)` | Opens the SQLite file selected by SchemaChecker |
| `genai/config/query.toml` | `tomllib.load()` at **import time** | Reads `enforce_default_limit`, `default_limit`, `max_display_rows` once |
| `genai/nl2sql_agent/state.py` | Reads + writes `AgentState` | Reads `sql_query`, `db_path`; writes `result_rows`, `result_columns`, `result_summary`, `error`, `stages` |
| `genai/utils/logger.py` | Python import | Logs SQL executed and row count |

**Read-only guarantee — what happens inside the connection:**

```
sqlite3.connect(db_path, check_same_thread=False)
conn.execute("BEGIN DEFERRED")   ← deferred = no write locks acquired
cursor = conn.execute(sql)       ← SELECT only (reviewed + validated upstream)
rows = cursor.fetchmany(500)     ← hard cap, never pulls the full table
conn.rollback()                  ← unconditional, even on exception
conn.close()
```

---

## Folder: `genai/utils/`

**What it is:** Shared infrastructure utilities. Every other folder imports from here;
nothing in `utils/` imports from `core/` or `nl2sql_agent/`.

### `genai/utils/config.py` — Settings Singleton

**Connects to:**

| Target | How | What happens |
|--------|-----|-------------|
| `.env` file (project root) | Pydantic `BaseSettings` auto-loads | API keys, URL overrides, log level loaded from environment / `.env` |
| `genai/config/*.toml` | Pydantic `BaseSettings` default values | TOML files provide committed defaults that `.env` can override |

`get_settings()` is called everywhere but instantiates `AppSettings` only once
at module import time (module-level singleton pattern).

---

### `genai/utils/logger.py` — Logging Factory

**Connects to:**

| Target | How | What happens |
|--------|-----|-------------|
| `genai/config/logging.toml` | `tomllib.load()` on first `get_logger()` call | `logging.config.dictConfig()` configures all handlers, formatters, and log levels |
| `data/logs/compliance_bot.log` | `RotatingFileHandler` (configured in `logging.toml`) | Log records are written here; file rotates at a configurable size |

Every module calls `get_logger("compliance_bot.<module_name>")`.
Logging is configured once; subsequent calls are no-ops.

---

### `genai/utils/registry.py` — Three-in-One Registry

This file merges three separate concerns that all share the same pattern:
**read a file once, cache it, return slices on demand**.

**1. Database metadata registry (from `databases.toml`)**

| Target | How | What happens |
|--------|-----|-------------|
| `genai/config/databases.toml` | `tomllib.load()` on first access | Parses the full table registry: descriptions, column definitions, few-shot strings, DB file paths |

Used by `SchemaChecker` (column descriptions) and `SQLWriterAgent` / `ContextBuilder`
(table description, column defs, few-shots).

**2. Read-only SQLite helpers**

| Target | How | What happens |
|--------|-----|-------------|
| Any `.db` file | `sqlite3.connect()` inside `open_readonly()` context manager | `BEGIN DEFERRED` → yield connection → `ROLLBACK` + `close()` |

Used by any code that needs to query a database without the full pipeline
(e.g. tests, standalone utilities).

**3. TOML prompt loader**

| Target | How | What happens |
|--------|-----|-------------|
| `genai/prompts/*.toml` | `tomllib.load()` on first `load_prompt(filename)` call | Parsed dict cached in `_prompt_cache`; subsequent calls return cached dict |

Used by every agent node that needs user-facing messages or prompt templates.

---

### `genai/utils/helpers.py` — Utility Functions

**Connects to:** Nothing. Pure standard-library utilities.

Used across `genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_writer_agent.py` (SQL clean-up),
`genai/core/processor.py` (JSON helpers), and anywhere that needs file I/O,
JSON parsing, or text manipulation.

---

## Folder: `genai/config/`

**What it is:** Committed TOML configuration files — no secrets, no code.

**Who reads each file:**

| File | Read by | Transport |
|------|---------|-----------|
| `application.toml` | `genai/utils/config.py` (Pydantic defaults) | Pydantic `BaseSettings` default factory |
| `database.toml` | `genai/utils/config.py` | Pydantic `BaseSettings` default factory |
| `databases.toml` | `genai/utils/registry.py` | `tomllib.load()` in `_build_db_registry()` |
| `llm.toml` | `genai/utils/config.py` | Pydantic `BaseSettings` default factory |
| `logging.toml` | `genai/utils/logger.py` | `tomllib.load()` + `logging.config.dictConfig()` |
| `query.toml` | `genai/nl2sql_agent/executor_agent.py` | `tomllib.load()` at module import time |
| `ui.toml` | `run_ui.py` / `application/run_ui.py` | Read at startup to determine backend URL |

---

## Folder: `genai/prompts/`

**What it is:** All user-facing strings and LLM prompt templates — no code, no secrets.

**Who reads each file:**

| File | Read by | Used for |
|------|---------|---------|
| `intent_checker.toml` | `intent_checker.py` via `registry.get_prompt()` | Clarification messages shown to users |
| `schema_checker.toml` | `schema_checker.py` via `registry.load_prompt()` / `get_prompt()` | Section header templates; `sample_row_limit` config |
| `sql_writer.toml` | `sql_writer_agent.py` via `registry.load_prompt()` | System role, rules list, full prompt template, retry feedback header |
| `sql_review.toml` | `sql_reviewer.py` via `registry.get_prompt()` | Corrective feedback strings sent to SQLWriterAgent on retry; user-facing error strings |
| `compliance.toml` | Legacy / future use | Compliance domain messages |
| `report_generation.toml` | Report pipeline (future) | Report output templates |
| `system.toml` | Shared messages | Global system-level messages |
| `common.toml` | Reference only | SQL delimiter tags documentation |

All files are read **once** at their first call and kept in `_prompt_cache`
(a module-level dict in `registry.py`). No disk I/O happens per request.

---

## Folder: `data/`

**What it is:** All runtime data, separated by purpose. Nothing in `data/` imports
Python code. All access is via `sqlite3` (read-only) or file writes from `logger.py`.

### `data/comp_db/`

**Who reads/writes:**

| File | Read by | Write by |
|------|---------|---------|
| `COMP_REPORTS.db` | `schema_checker.py` (PRAGMA introspection + sample rows); `sql_reviewer.py` (table existence check); `executor_agent.py` (SELECT query execution) | `data/seed_synthetic_data.py` (data loading script, run manually) |

All reads are via `sqlite3` in either `BEGIN DEFERRED` mode (nodes 2, 4, 5) or
plain `sqlite3.connect()` (Node 2 scoring in `_score()`).

### `data/logs/`

**Who writes:** `genai/utils/logger.py` via Python's `RotatingFileHandler`.
**Who reads:** Operators / DevOps (tail the file for monitoring).

### Other `data/` sub-folders

| Folder | Purpose | Currently active |
|--------|---------|-----------------|
| `data/uploads/` | User file uploads (CSV / Excel ingestion) | Placeholder (`.gitkeep`) |
| `data/reports/` | Generated compliance report outputs | Placeholder |
| `data/exports/` | CSV exports from the Executor | Placeholder |
| `data/cache/` | Query or response caching | Placeholder |
| `data/temp/` | Temporary processing files | Placeholder |
| `data/sample_data/` | Reference / seed data for testing | Placeholder |

---

## Folder: `tests/`

**What it is:** pytest test suite covering all layers.

**Connects to:**

| Test file | Tests |
|-----------|-------|
| `test_fastapi_app.py` | FastAPI endpoints via `TestClient`; mocks `_compliance_processor` |
| `test_compliance_processor.py` | `ComplianceProcessor` with a mock compiled graph |
| `test_intent_agent.py` | `IntentChecker.run()` — all four check branches |
| `test_schema_agent.py` | `SchemaChecker.run()` + `_score()` + `_build_context()` with in-memory SQLite |
| `test_sql_review_agent.py` | `SQLReviewer.run()` — all check branches and retry logic |
| `test_sql_validator.py` | `SQLValidator.run()` — parse success, parse failure, non-SELECT |
| `test_executor_agent.py` | `ExecutorAgent.run()` + `_execute_read_only()` with real in-memory SQLite |
| `test_db_registry.py` | `registry._build_db_registry()`, `get_table_meta()`, `get_few_shots()` |
| `test_schemas.py` | Pydantic model validation and factory functions |
| `test_utils.py` | `helpers.py` utility functions |
| `conftest.py` | Shared fixtures (temp DB files, mock model service, …) |

Tests do **not** require a real LLM provider or the actual `COMP_REPORTS.db` file.
`ModelLoaderService.generate()` is mocked in all tests that touch Node 3.

---

## Folder: `docs/`

**What it is:** This documentation. Markdown files only — no code.

| File | Covers |
|------|--------|
| `index.md` | Documentation hub — quick navigation |
| `architecture.md` | Deep technical architecture (full pipeline, state, config, exceptions) |
| `folder-connections.md` | **This file** — how every folder connects to every other folder |
| `how-to-use.md` | Quick start, running the app, example questions |
| `adding-new-database.md` | Step-by-step guide to adding a new SQLite database and table (TOML only) |
| `configuration.md` | All TOML config files, `.env` variables, row-limit settings |
| `database-schema.md` | Full `COMP_TBL` column reference (22 columns) |
| `api-reference.md` | REST endpoints, request/response schemas, curl examples |
| `testing.md` | Running pytest, coverage targets, writing new tests |

---

## Complete Import Dependency Graph

```
application/main.py
    ├── genai.core.exceptions
    ├── genai.core.model          (ModelLoaderService)
    ├── genai.core.processor      (ComplianceProcessor)
    ├── genai.core.schemas        (request/response Pydantic models)
    ├── genai.nl2sql_agent.graph  (build_nl2sql_graph)
    ├── genai.utils.config        (get_settings, ModelSettings)
    └── genai.utils.logger        (get_logger)

genai/core/processor.py
    ├── genai.core.exceptions
    ├── genai.core.schemas
    ├── genai.nl2sql_agent.state  (AgentState)
    └── genai.utils.logger

genai/core/model.py
    ├── genai.core.exceptions
    ├── genai.utils.config        (ModelSettings, SQL_BEGIN/END_TAG, defaults)
    └── genai.utils.logger

genai/nl2sql_agent/graph.py
    ├── genai.core.model          (ModelLoaderService)
    ├── genai.nl2sql_agent.*      (all five node classes)
    ├── genai.nl2sql_agent.state  (AgentState)
    ├── genai.utils.config        (get_settings)
    └── genai.utils.logger

genai/nl2sql_agent/intent_checker.py
    ├── genai.nl2sql_agent.state
    ├── genai.utils.logger
    └── genai.utils.registry      (get_prompt)

genai/nl2sql_agent/schema_checker.py
    ├── genai.nl2sql_agent.state
    ├── genai.utils.logger
    └── genai.utils.registry      (get_prompt, get_table_meta, load_prompt)
    [+ sqlite3 I/O on data/comp_db/]

genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_writer_agent.py
    ├── genai.core.model          (ModelLoaderService)
    ├── genai.nl2sql_agent.state
    ├── genai.utils.config        (COMPLIANCE_TABLE_NAME, SQL_BEGIN/END_TAG)
    ├── genai.utils.logger
    └── genai.utils.registry      (load_prompt, get_prompt, get_table_meta,
                                   get_few_shots, get_all_tables, list_databases)
    [+ HTTP I/O to DLH / ICA]

genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_reviewer.py
    ├── genai.nl2sql_agent.state
    ├── genai.utils.logger
    └── genai.utils.registry      (get_prompt)
    [+ sqlite3 I/O on data/comp_db/ for table existence check]

genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_validator.py
    ├── genai.nl2sql_agent.state
    └── genai.utils.logger
    [+ sqlglot package (optional)]

genai/nl2sql_agent/executor_agent.py
    ├── genai.nl2sql_agent.state
    └── genai.utils.logger
    [+ tomllib I/O on genai/config/query.toml at import time]
    [+ sqlite3 I/O on data/comp_db/ per request]

genai/utils/config.py
    └── [reads .env + Pydantic defaults; no genai imports]

genai/utils/logger.py
    └── [reads genai/config/logging.toml; no genai imports]

genai/utils/registry.py
    └── genai.utils.logger
    [+ tomllib I/O on genai/config/databases.toml (once, cached)]
    [+ tomllib I/O on genai/prompts/*.toml (once per file, cached)]
    [+ sqlite3 I/O via open_readonly() (on demand)]

genai/utils/helpers.py
    └── [stdlib only — no genai imports]
```

---

## Key Rules About What Can Import What

| Rule | Reason |
|------|--------|
| `genai/utils/` **never** imports from `genai/core/` or `genai/nl2sql_agent/` | `utils/` is shared infrastructure; circular imports would break the entire tree |
| `genai/core/` **never** imports from `genai/nl2sql_agent/` | `core/` holds the LLM client and schemas; the pipeline nodes are a higher-level concern |
| `genai/nl2sql_agent/` nodes **never** import from each other | Each node is isolated; they communicate only through `AgentState` |
| `application/` imports from `genai/` but `genai/` does **not** import from `application/` | Clean dependency direction: server layer depends on business logic, not the other way round |
| `data/` contains **no Python imports** | Data layer is pure files; all access is via stdlib `sqlite3` or file handles |
| `docs/` contains **no Python imports** | Documentation layer is pure Markdown |

FILE: docs\how-to-use.md
####################################################################################################

# How to Use

## Quick Start

### 1. Prerequisites

- Python 3.11 or higher
- Access to a **DLH** on-prem inference server
- Git (to clone the repository)

### 2. Install dependencies

```bash
pip install -r requirements.txt
```

> **Python < 3.11** also requires `tomli` for TOML parsing:
> ```bash
> pip install tomli
> ```

### 3. Configure environment

```bash
cp .env.example .env
```

Edit `.env` and set your DLH credentials:

```env
MODEL__PROVIDER=dlh
MODEL__MODEL_NAME=google/gemma-4-31b-it
MODEL__DLH_BASE_URL=http://your-dlh-host:9000/api/v1
MODEL__DLH_API_KEY=EMPTY
```

### 4. Seed the database

The database is **not** auto-populated. Run the seeder once before first use:

```bash
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset --count 2000
```

This creates `data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db` with 2,000 realistic banking rows.

| Option | Description |
|---|---|
| `--reset` | Wipe the table before inserting |
| `--count N` | Number of rows to generate |
| `--seed N` | Fixed random seed for reproducibility |

### 5. Start the application

```bash
# Recommended — starts backend + UI proxy together
python application/Launcher.py
```

The launcher will:
1. Auto-detect a free port near `8001` for the backend
2. Auto-detect a free port near `8100` for the UI
3. Poll `/health` until the backend is ready (up to 60 s)
4. Open the UI server

```
==============================================================
 SYSTEMS STARTED
   Backend  (FastAPI):  http://127.0.0.1:8001
   Frontend (UI proxy): http://127.0.0.1:8100
==============================================================
```

Open **http://127.0.0.1:8100** in your browser.

---

## Running Options

### Option A — Launcher (recommended for CDSW / CML)

```bash
python application/Launcher.py
```

Starts both processes, monitors them, and prints exit codes if either crashes.

**Environment variables:**

| Variable | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `PORT` | `8001` | FastAPI backend port (auto-increments if busy) |
| `CDSW_APP_PORT` | `8100` | UI proxy port (auto-increments if busy) |

### Option B — Backend only

```bash
python application/main.py
# or
python -m uvicorn application.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 --reload
```

Serves the FastAPI backend and Swagger docs at `http://localhost:8000/docs`.

### Option C — Standalone UI proxy

```bash
CDSW_APP_PORT=8100 python run_ui.py
```

The standalone UI is a pure-stdlib HTTP server that:
- Serves the embedded single-page application
- Proxies all `/api/v1/*` and `/health` calls to the FastAPI backend
- Opens the browser automatically

> Requires `CDSW_APP_PORT` to be set, and `CB_BACKEND_URL` to point to the running backend.

### Option D — Direct uvicorn (production)

```bash
uvicorn application.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 --workers 4 --log-level info
```

### Option E — Pipeline CLI (debug)

```bash
python -m genai.nl2sql_agent.main "How many senior citizens are in Mumbai?"
```

Runs the full pipeline from the command line and prints JSON results. Useful for debugging without a browser.

---

## Using the UI

### LLM Configuration page

1. Fill in the Model Name, DLH Base URL, and DLH API Key
2. Click **Test & Save** — this tests the connection and rebuilds the LangGraph pipeline
3. The stage pills at the top of the chat page light up as each node completes

### Chat page

Type a compliance question in the message box and press **Enter** (or **Send**).

**Example questions:**

```
How many accounts have KYC flag N?
Show all senior citizen accounts in the Mumbai circle.
What is the average balance of accounts with maturity date in 2025?
List the top 10 accounts by end-of-day balance.
How many accounts have the product name containing 'SR CT'?
Show accounts where age is above 60 and KYC is not compliant.
Count accounts by circle name.
What is the total balance for branches in the Thane circle?
```

### Understanding the response

Each answer shows:

| Section | Description |
|---|---|
| **Agent Trace** | `intent_agent:ok → schema_agent:db=... → sql_writer_agent:... → ...` |
| **Summary** | Human-readable result: scalar value, row count, or "no matching records" |
| **SQL** | The exact SQLite query that was executed |
| **Rows returned** | Count of result rows |
| **Results** | Full result set as JSON |
| **Download as CSV** | Downloads the result set as a CSV file |

### Clarification cards

- **Blue card** — the question was too vague; rephrase with more specific keywords
- **Red card** — the question or generated SQL contained a write keyword (DELETE/DROP/…)

---

## Understanding the Row Limit

By default, queries with no explicit `LIMIT` clause are automatically capped at **10 rows**.

To get more rows, either:
- Ask *"show top 50 accounts by balance"* — the LLM will include `LIMIT 50`
- Or change `default_limit` in `genai/config/query.toml`

See [configuration.md → query.toml](configuration.md#querytom) for full details.

---

## API Usage (curl)

### Health check

```bash
curl http://localhost:8000/health
```

```json
{"status": "ok", "version": "2.0.0"}
```

### Configure LLM

```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/api/v1/config/llm \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{
    "provider": "dlh",
    "model_name": "google/gemma-4-31b-it",
    "dlh_base_url": "http://your-host:9000/api/v1",
    "dlh_api_key": "EMPTY"
  }'
```

### Ask a compliance question (full orchestration)

```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/api/v1/compliance/orchestrate \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"question": "How many persons from the Mumbai circle?"}'
```

See [api-reference.md](api-reference.md) for full response schemas.

---

## Swagger / OpenAPI

Interactive API documentation is available at:

- **Swagger UI**: `http://localhost:8000/docs`
- **ReDoc**: `http://localhost:8000/redoc`

# Testing

## Running tests

```bash
# Run all tests
pytest

# Verbose output
pytest -v

# With coverage report
pytest --cov=genai --cov-report=term-missing

# Specific test file
pytest tests/test_intent.py -v

# Specific test function
pytest tests/test_intent.py::test_empty_question -v
```

---

## Coverage targets

| Package | Target |
|---|---|
| `genai/nl2sql_agent/` | ≥ 90% |
| `genai/core/` | ≥ 90% |
| `genai/utils/` | ≥ 85% |

---

## Test file layout

```
tests/
├── __init__.py
├── test_intent.py          # IntentAgent — 4 checks
├── test_schema.py          # SchemaAgent — DB scoring, schema context
├── test_sql_review.py      # SQLReviewAgent — safety + table checks
├── test_sql_validator.py   # SQLValidatorAgent — sqlglot AST
├── test_executor.py        # ExecutorAgent — read-only, limit policy
├── test_db_registry.py     # db_registry — TOML loading
├── test_compliance_processor.py  # End-to-end (mocked graph)
└── conftest.py             # Shared fixtures
```

---

## Writing tests

### Testing deterministic nodes (no LLM)

All nodes except `SQLWriterAgent` are fully deterministic and can be tested without mocking:

```python
from genai.nl2sql_agent.intent import IntentAgent
from genai.nl2sql_agent.state import AgentState

def test_empty_question():
    agent = IntentAgent()
    result = agent.run(AgentState(question="", stages=[]))  # type: ignore
    assert result["intent_ok"] is False
    assert "intent_agent:empty" in result["stages"]

def test_disallowed_action_word():
    agent = IntentAgent()
    result = agent.run(AgentState(question="delete all records", stages=[]))  # type: ignore
    assert result["intent_ok"] is False
    assert any("disallowed" in s for s in result["stages"])

def test_valid_question():
    agent = IntentAgent()
    result = agent.run(AgentState(question="How many KYC non-compliant accounts?", stages=[]))  # type: ignore
    assert result["intent_ok"] is True
    assert result["stages"] == ["intent_agent:ok"]
```

### Testing SQLWriterAgent (requires mocked LLM)

```python
from unittest.mock import MagicMock
from genai.nl2sql_agent.sql_writer_agent import SQLWriterAgent
from genai.nl2sql_agent.state import AgentState

def test_sql_writer_extracts_sql():
    model_service = MagicMock()
    model_service.generate.return_value = (
        "<begin_sql_response>\nSELECT COUNT(*) FROM COMP_TBL;\n<end_sql_response>"
    )
    model_service.extract_sql.return_value = "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM COMP_TBL;"

    agent = SQLWriterAgent(model_service=model_service)
    state = AgentState(  # type: ignore
        interpreted_question="How many records are there?",
        schema_context="## Database: COMP_REPORTS\n...",
        retry_count=0,
        stages=[],
    )
    result = agent.run(state)
    assert result["sql_query"] == "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM COMP_TBL;"
    assert "sql_writer_agent:llm_generated retry=0" in result["stages"]
```

### Testing the executor with a real database

```python
import sqlite3
import tempfile
from pathlib import Path
from genai.nl2sql_agent.executor import ExecutorAgent
from genai.nl2sql_agent.state import AgentState

def test_executor_read_only(tmp_path):
    db = tmp_path / "test.db"
    with sqlite3.connect(str(db)) as conn:
        conn.execute("CREATE TABLE T (id INTEGER, name TEXT)")
        conn.execute("INSERT INTO T VALUES (1, 'Alice')")
        conn.execute("INSERT INTO T VALUES (2, 'Bob')")
        conn.commit()

    agent = ExecutorAgent()
    state = AgentState(sql_query="SELECT * FROM T", db_path=str(db), stages=[])  # type: ignore
    result = agent.run(state)

    assert result["error"] is None
    assert len(result["result_rows"]) <= 10   # default_limit from query.toml
    assert result["result_columns"] == ["id", "name"]
```

### Testing db_registry

```python
from genai.utils.db_registry import get_table_meta, get_few_shots, get_all_tables

def test_comp_tbl_registered():
    meta = get_table_meta("COMP_TBL")
    assert meta["description"] != ""
    assert "KYC_FLG" in meta["columns"]
    assert "CIRCLENAME" in meta["columns"]

def test_few_shots_present():
    shots = get_few_shots("COMP_TBL")
    assert "<begin_sql_response>" in shots

def test_unknown_table_returns_empty():
    meta = get_table_meta("NON_EXISTENT_TABLE")
    assert meta == {}
```

### conftest.py — shared fixtures

```python
import pytest
import sqlite3
from pathlib import Path

@pytest.fixture
def sample_db(tmp_path) -> Path:
    """Create a minimal in-memory-like SQLite database for testing."""
    db = tmp_path / "COMP_REPORTS.db"
    with sqlite3.connect(str(db)) as conn:
        conn.execute("""
            CREATE TABLE COMP_TBL (
                BRCD TEXT,
                CUST_NBR TEXT,
                KYC_FLG TEXT,
                AGE_YRS INTEGER,
                SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG INTEGER,
                END_OF_DAY_BAL REAL,
                CIRCLENAME TEXT
            )
        """)
        conn.executemany(
            "INSERT INTO COMP_TBL VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)",
            [
                ("1001", "C001", "Y", 65, 1, 50000.0, "MUMBAI"),
                ("1001", "C002", "N", 45, 0, 30000.0, "MUMBAI"),
                ("1002", "C003", "Y", 70, 1, 80000.0, "THANE"),
            ],
        )
        conn.commit()
    return db
```

---

## Running tests in CI

Add a `pytest.ini` or `pyproject.toml` section:

```toml
# pyproject.toml
[tool.pytest.ini_options]
testpaths  = ["tests"]
addopts    = "-v --tb=short"
asyncio_mode = "auto"
```

GitHub Actions example:

```yaml
- name: Run tests
  run: |
    pip install -r requirements.txt
    pytest --cov=genai --cov-report=xml
```

# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# databases.toml — database and table registry
#
# HOW TO ADD A NEW DATABASE / TABLE
# ──────────────────────────────────
# 1. Add a new [databases.<DB_STEM>] block with db_file and description.
# 2. Add one [databases.<DB_STEM>.tables.<TABLE_NAME>] block per table with:
#      description   — plain-English summary used in the LLM prompt
#      filter_query  — optional parameterised filter template
# 3. Add [databases.<DB_STEM>.tables.<TABLE_NAME>.columns] with
#      COLUMN_NAME = "Human-readable description"
#    for every column the LLM should know about.
# 4. Add [databases.<DB_STEM>.tables.<TABLE_NAME>.few_shots] with
#      examples = """..."""  (Question/Response pairs, one per block)
#
# NO Python code changes are needed — the pipeline picks up new entries
# automatically on next startup.
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────


# ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

[databases.COMP_REPORTS.tables.COMP_TBL.few_shots]

# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# query.toml — row-limit and query execution settings
#
# enforce_default_limit
#   true  → when the LLM-generated SQL has no LIMIT clause, automatically
#            append "LIMIT <default_limit>" before executing.
#   false → execute the SQL as-is, no automatic LIMIT is added.
#
# default_limit
#   Number of rows to show when no LIMIT is present in the SQL and
#   enforce_default_limit = true.
#
# max_display_rows
#   Hard ceiling on rows returned to the API caller regardless of any LIMIT
#   in the SQL.  Acts as a safety net.  Set to 0 to disable.
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

[query]
enforce_default_limit = false
default_limit         = 10
max_display_rows      = 50
